o-----------------------------------o
| Pokémon Altered Platinum          |
| Pokémon Changes (r1.0.5)          |
| Original hack by Drayano          |
o-----------------------------------o

=========================================================
General Changes
=========================================================
- The Fairy-type has been added to the game, replacing the almost unused ???-type.
- All Pokémon have had their base stats updated to match Ultra Sun and Ultra Moon.
- All Pokémon have had their types updated to match Ultra Sun and Ultra Moon.
- All Pokémon have had their wild held item updated to match Ultra Sun and Ultra Moon (with some exceptions).
- All Pokémon now have an egg cycle of 0, i.e. they will hatch extremely quickly.
- Wingull, Pelipper and Torkoal have gained their new abilities from Gen VII.

- Pokémon that evolved with items which could no longer learn moves on level up now have full level-up sets.
  This was changed since I personally never liked this mechanic, as it meant you might have to wait a long time
  with an underpowered Pokémon just to get the move you want. This is dissimilar to the Pokémon which previously
  evolved with held items during a trade, which can use their items now with no strings attached.
  I feel consistency with these Pokémon is the best option, and it makes them easier to train.

=========================================================
Effort Value Removal
=========================================================
Effort Values are completely gone. Just entirely absent.
This was done for a variety of reasons- the difficulty of this hack is intended to be based on tactical decisions,
teambuilding and things like that- not on being a munchkin and trying to create the ultimate life-form.
As well, opposing trainers can't use EVs anyway, so it's more balanced this way. You no longer have to worry about
accidentally EV training your Jolteon with Attack and HP EVs, and such. IVs and natures are comparatively less
impactful, and with Synchronize desirable natures can still be acquired if you really want them.

With non-optimal IVs/Natures, your Pokémon have a marginal disadvantage, but the AI, even at max level, isn't the smartest
anyway, so it can be thought of as a sort of handicap to make the trainers more dangerous.

=========================================================
Held Item Exceptions
=========================================================
- Pokémon who may hold an item in Ultra Sun and Ultra Moon that doesn't exist in Platinum will not hold anything.
- Corsola has a 5% chance to hold a Hard Stone.
- Pelipper has a 5% chance to hold a Lucky Egg.
- Sinnohan Absol has a 5% chance to hold a Life Orb.
- Spiritomb has a 100% chance to hold a Smoke Ball.

=========================================================
Other Changes
=========================================================
- Some Pokémon have ability slots flipped (e.g. Scizor). This is because enemy Trainers always use the first ability.
- The rate for Shiny Pokémon is now 1/512, instead of 1/8192.
- Many of the shiny Pokémon I thought looked hideous have had entirely new palettes added.
  Some I've changed just because I wanted to, but a large majority of shinies are unchanged. Either because I doubt most
  people care about them, or because I already like them enough.
  All starters except Venusaur, Charizard and Meganium have new shinies, though!
  Also- Onix and Steelix are based on the Crystal Onix from the anime!

=========================================================
Ability changes
=========================================================
Several abilities have been renamed so that they can apply to more Pokémon. The purpose of this is to increase ability variety,
by adding 'new' clone abilities(like Huge Power/Pure Power) with a more general concept, so more Pokémon can benefit.
Generation 4 has a lack of abilities I would like to implement if it was possible, so this is a nice compromise.
All of these abilities do exactly the same thing- the rename is only meant to make them fit on more Pokémon.

Clear Body has been renamed to Strong Body.
New description: "Its toughness prevents stat reduction."
New users: Hitmonchan, Pinsir, Heracross, Skarmory, Whiscash, Armaldo, Piplup/Prinplup/Empoleon, Snover

Download has been renamed to Instinct.
New description: "Boosts an offense based on foe's weakness."
New users: Nidoking, Voltorb/Electrode, Sceptile, Kecleon, Deoxys, Mothim, Lucario, Toxicroak, Sinnohan Furret

Compound Eyes has been renamed to Deadeye.
New description: "Amazing senses boost its accuracy."
New users: Pidgeot, Seel, Dewgong, Starmie, Persian, Kecleon, Sinnohan Scyther, Sinnohan Flygon

Rock Head has been renamed to Determined.
New description: "Its sheer determination prevents recoil damage."
New users: Charizard, Tauros, Flareon, Ursaring, Blaziken

Own Tempo has been renamed to Simple Mind.
New description: "Its clear thoughts prevent confusion."
New users: Primeape

Sturdy has been entirely removed from the game, as one-hit KO moves no longer exist.
Lightning Rod and Storm Drain have also been entirely removed from the game. This is because in Gen 4, they lack their
Electric/Water immunity attributes, as well as the Special Attack boost, and ONLY redirect these moves during double battles,
making them ludicrously terrible. Most instances have been replaced with Volt Absorb/Motor Drive and Water Absorb.

=========================================================
Custom Changes
=========================================================
From the bases as listed above, further changes to any Pokémon are listed below. These can include changes to base stats, abilities, types etcetera.

Some Pokémon have had a Hidden Ability from a later generation added as a regular ability (e.g. Torkoal).

TM compatibilty with any of the new TMs (Bug Buzz, Hurricane) is also listed here.

Any move previously unavailable to a Pokémon is listed with a (!!) besides it.
If a move was added to a Pokémon because of Pokémon Legends: Arceus, it will be listed with a (PLA) besides it.

Level up moves have also been changed for every single Pokémon. These should include all moves they learn in Ultra Sun and Ultra Moon that are available in Altered Platinum, as well as additional moves from move tutors, egg moves or entirely new techniques.

Each Pokémon is listed under its own section header below.
